| Object and linking (§§9.1–9.3) | One ISA object's closed sections and linked symbols; no native-container schema. | Publish a versioned inventory associating embedded/linked ISA object bytes and object_hash with selected device roots and certificates. A native-container hash, if used, is a separate identity. |
| Certificates/inventory (§§12.1–12.3) | Closure-keyed device-region records; current text does not explicitly delimit native inventory. | Insert the shared scoping rule in §12.3, cross-reference from §9.2, and require complete device-owner/link-proof coverage per root. Multiple device roots can share static closures without merging root-relative contexts. |
| Launch/entry (§10) | bennett_run(program, cc_entry, args, entropy) starts device CC; it is not native main. | Publish a host call/return shim and container/root selection/feature negotiation. Define native argument/result marshalling, including ptr (whose host binding §10 does not specify), status/fault propagation, suspend/reverse/close and ownership. Do not invent a current general launch shim. |
| Budgets (§5.4; §§10–11) | Validated token tuples and identity scoped to a program/run; action_call has no primary token (§4.7). | Bind selected device invocation budgets and token equality to the launch request; charge actual device/replay work. State host/shim accounting separately; a native process has no invented global B. |
| Handles (§3.3; §10; dialect §6.6) | Register ids refer to the loaded program's ledger; run ownership and token validity are explicit. | Map host-visible opaque handles to that program/ledger with validity and cross-launch ownership checks; preserve class vectors and token identities. Do not serialize native pointers as device ids or token tuples as identities. |
| Effect tape (§7.3; §10) | Run-associated R effects and explicit copied hostcall requests; no implicit log of native host I/O. | Associate each device run and tape with the launched root and define host effect/proxy ordering and replay at the boundary, preserving §7.4 reversal. Saved-journal interchange needs its own versioned replay profile. |
| Host memory by reference (§§7.1–7.2; dialect §§8.5, 17.1, 19.4–19.5) | Device layout plus dialect snapshot/replay and boundary-address/promotion rules; copied hostcall bytes do not provide general shared pointers. | Publish the mapping and lifetime protocol named in the statement, including allocator metadata/history and pointer-rich layouts, so ordinary host memory can be supplied safely without universal host promotion. Retain existing snapshot/replay as a construction, not the sole eventual capability. |

For example native main can call marked roots A and B in distinct ISA objects.
Each has its own object/root certificate and root context []; neither
certificate inventories main or host-only libc. A shared linked device
callee is checked in each root's actual invocation tree. A native comparison
executes the corresponding materialised region/callee closure under its
recorded layout, input and tool configuration (dialect §§17.7, 19.5, 19.9).

These notes specify boundary scope and exact remaining contract work; they
implement no container, host shim or memory registration. The existing
bennett-4ht issue tracks that publication/implementation obligation. Current
device contracts and their complete estimate/proof requirements remain in
force; an unimplemented boundary is not evidence that mixed execution works.


# The document under review (round 1 snapshot)

# b-isa-spec-2qc — checker duty for qinplace coverage

Council proposal. References: [ISA 0.9](../../../spec/Bennett-ISA-Spec-0.9.md)
§4.5 K4, §§9.2–9.3, §12.1 “Invocation contexts”, §12.3 “Invocation
binding (K13)”, §16.3 example 7; related
[0jq owner proposal](../2026-11-02-pass2/b-isa-spec-0jq.md).

## Exact defect and North Star scope

§4.5 already requires an inverse-pair entry in the certificate record
of the region whose body contains qinplace. An unordered pair of exact
g/ginv closure hashes covers either orientation. Coverage also requires
entry.budget ≤ tk componentwise, exact token-argument tuples and
membership of the other arguments in the claim's args domain. The
executor must preflight every transitively executed qinplace before
any effect of a CC/R region call, or trap/fault 4.

§12.1 specifies verification of an entry's claim, and §12.3 specifies
binding resolution, but neither explicitly turns **coverage of sites**
into a checker duty. Validating a supplied inverse-pair proof and
checking that every applicable instruction has one are different tasks.
The request exposes this checker/executor responsibility ambiguity,
not a missing executor trap, inverse algorithm or proof owner. Resolving
all three questions needs static pair presence, bound applicability and
nested-context scope, rather than just “check inverse_pairs”.

North Star §§3.1, 4.2, 5.1 promises checked in-place bijections and honest
proof/evidence labels. Requiring those existing obligations at certificate
acceptance preserves that promise. A standalone proof with unresolved
call parameters may remain useful; it must not be labeled executable
acceptance or be rejected merely for lacking a schedule it never claims.

## Alternatives and concrete witnesses

Use §16.3 example 7, owner `addya`, with qinplace G/GINV, token tk and
classical argument a. The entry proves both compositions on the full
x,y domain, minimum tuple [3,0,0,0], a in 0..3.

| Case | Proposed checker result |
|---|---|
| No entry, or only {G,G} for a {G,GINV} site | Reject missing owner-local pair. |
| tk=[3,0,0,0], a=1, valid full-domain claim | Covered. |
| tk=[9,0,0,0], same a | Covered by primary-token monotonicity. |
| tk=[2,0,0,0] | Reject applicability for that bound invocation. |
| a=3 but the claim covers only 0..2 | Reject applicability. |
| G/GINV swapped in the entry | Covered by the same two-composition proof. |
| Correct entry, no resolvable standalone invocation | Report coverage unestablished; no executable-acceptance conclusion. |

For nesting, outer calls inner twice; inner owns the qinplace. Its tk
actuals are 3 then 2. An entry with minimum 3 covers the first context
and misses the second, even if both have the same variant/control count
and outer's root budget is 9. An entry in outer's claims array cannot
replace inner's. If a qinplace forwards a token argument k, an entry's
arg_tokens.k=[3,0,0,0] does not cover k=[4,0,0,0], however large tk is.

| Alternative | Consequence |
|---|---|
| Executor-only coverage | Safe execution still traps, but a checker can advertise an unusable certificate; requires an explicit limited verdict. |
| Check pair presence and root only | Catches common errors, misses the second inner context above. |
| Check owner presence plus every bound context | Aligns checker acceptance and existing preflight; recommended. |
| Require a universal proof for all possible calls of every standalone record | Unnecessarily rejects useful invocation-scoped/parameter-uniform claims. |

## Recommended exact spec diff

Insert after §12.3 Invocation binding's paragraph ending “not a target
realisation”, before CLAIM-BINDING witness:

```text
Qinplace coverage checking. For every qinplace source site in a supplied
region record, the checker MUST require an inverse_pairs entry in that
owner region's record for the exact pair of resolved g/ginv closure
hashes, in either orientation, and MUST reject a missing pair or invalid
referenced claim. Resolve imported owner records and library claims by
§9.3; indices name claims in their owner record, never in an outer caller.

When a root invocation is bound by bindings.invocation (or by a
standalone claim whose subject is that root), the checker MUST apply
§4.5's complete
covering test at every reached qinplace site in that invocation and its
transitive invocation contexts, rejecting any uncovered site. With a
schedule, use §12.1's complete realised invocation tree, including
default-covered calls, iterations and source-adjoint/control contexts;
without a schedule, use the bound reference invocation tree. Resolve
the site's tk and args from that context, not the root's tuple reused
for all callees. A disabled coherent predicate does not remove an
entered instruction's obligation. Entry claims must be established by
their specified exhaustive or linked-library rule; sampled agreement
does not cover. Do not treat two private generated oracle calls as new
owners of the containing source site's entry.

A standalone record with an entry but no resolvable invocation may
retain its established standalone claims. The checker MUST report its
qinplace applicability as unestablished and MUST NOT conclude executable
acceptance from it. Universal applicability may instead be established
by a checked parameter-uniform argument covering every admitted call.
If a required invocation, linked body or context cannot be resolved or
checked, report that obligation unestablished; an implementation work
limit is not proof of absence or applicability. Malformed bindings and
missing required pairs still reject. No successful verdict may imply
that an unestablished obligation was discharged.

These checks do not remove the executor's §4.5 pre-effect trap/fault
requirement. A verified result may be reused only for the closure,
entry proof and invocation/domain it actually covers; closure equality
alone does not establish applicability at another token/args binding.
```

“Every source site” makes the existing carried-entry requirement explicit
even for a zero-iteration source loop. Context-dependent applicability
is tested only at reached sites. This separates static inventory from
call-dependent coverage; it does not require executing an unreachable
quantum branch as a classical branch or searching all possible CC inputs.
The driver/executor supplies the actual root binding before use.

## Hashes, catalog and consumers

No source envelope, public signature, ref catalog or instruction expansion
changes. Correcting a missing entry changes certificate bytes and any
certificate digest. Wrongly accepted executable packets may now reject
or become explicitly incomplete; this is a compatibility decision, not
an invisible checker patch. Composition must retain each owner's claims
and root-relative contexts; a cache keyed only by closure cannot cache
call coverage. VM, specialiser, producer and independent checker owners
need the same applicability vectors. Fault code 4 and pre-effect timing
are already normative and do not change.

## Conformance vectors and actual verification

Ran the existing independent replay's `QinplaceCoverage` (eight tests)
with K13Witness (four), using [INDEX](INDEX.md)'s bounded command: exit 0,
12 passed. These exercise absent/wrong pairs, swapped orientation,
larger/shorter tk, args range and unbound handling. They do not exercise
the proposed complete nested traversal. The replay explicitly reports
non-root applicability unchecked. Its verdict is not a newly ratified
contract. The published checker's `docs/checker.md` separately states
that verification-index shapes do not discharge qinplace applicability.

Acceptance adds the two-inner-context witness, default-covered and
adjoint calls, qloop 0 versus repeated visits, inherited coherent control,
outer-only entry rejection, imported owner/library records, exact token
argument mismatch and sampled-claim noncoverage. For the executor check
fault 4 occurs before any region effect and preserves the reversible
prefix. A valid structural-only standalone claim must survive with
coverage explicitly unestablished. The finite INDEX predicate checks
the binding distinctions, not a new complete certificate checker.

## K entry and publication route

Recommend an explicit **0.9 K checker-obligation repair**, coordinated
with 0jq, if council authorizes the existing repair track. Proposed row:
`K(new) | §§4.5,12.3 | QINPLACE-CHECKER-COVERAGE: owner-local pair
presence plus all bound transitive contexts; unbound applicability is
unestablished; executor preflight retained | 2qc,0jq | checker semantics`.
Reserve no numeric K identifier. Under the published semantic-version
rule this belongs in **0.10** unless the council explicitly authorizes
an in-version repair; see [INDEX](INDEX.md). Keep 2qc open for that
decision and consumer implementations.

## Mixed fat object

[fat-object-scope.md](../2026-11-02-rev2/fat-object-scope.md) applies (North
Star §§2, 3.3, 4.4). Coverage begins at each selected embedded device
root, context [], and uses its linked owner records. Two native calls to
that root can bind different tk/args; neither inherits coverage merely
from the other's certificate. Native main, host-only libc and other
device roots need no artificial region inventory. The container-to-root
association, launch budget/handle negotiation and tape/run association
remain the exact boundary changes named in the shared note. This proposal
checks device invocations without requiring whole-binary lowering.

I’ll check the cited clauses, proposed checker rules, and reproduction evidence against ISA 0.9 before grading. I’m using the repository’s Beads guidance for read-only issue context.

# Contract derivations 3 — council decision index

Five proposals, not published spec changes or consumer acceptance. Read
COMMON, repository CLAUDE/AGENTS, the Beads skill/prime, two first-pass
notes and two pass2 notes before derivation. All five full issue records
were read with bd. Direct sibling reads, including --readonly, failed
at the read-only embedded Dolt LOCK. Copies of the actual embedded
databases under `.tmp/sol-derive3/` then permitted `bd --readonly show`
for 4yg.1 and brt-vbi. No passive JSONL was imported or substituted for
the database. Source issues remain open for council/implementation work.

| Issue | Determination | Recommended publication |
|---|---|---|
| [bennett-arith-4yg.1](bennett-arith-4yg.1.md) | Valid FMA and binary128-conversion midpoints have no even candidate under the printed PPC rule; flags separately violate an existing rule | Dialect §13.1 parity repair in next 0.2 patch and 0.3 draft; no ISA K |
| [brt-vbi](brt-vbi.md) | Published all-width helper requirements lack a public delivery/collection protocol; actual i257 bodies also missing | Dialect §§12.8/19.1 offline interface and fixed point; no new ISA opcode/K |
| [b-isa-spec-pe1](b-isa-spec-pe1.md) | K13 example invents a borrowed lifetime that K70 rejects | 0.9 K13 example erratum: empty borrowed arrays, 24-bit D |
| [b-isa-spec-2qc](b-isa-spec-2qc.md) | Executor preflight and proof owner exist; certificate checker coverage duty is ambiguous | Explicit owner/site/context rule, coordinated with 0jq; council-authorized 0.9 K repair or 0.10 |
| [b-isa-spec-sqr](b-isa-spec-sqr.md) | Bundle entries in an mcx list have no explicit operand rule; current implementations disagree | Ordered bundle expansion, full wire arity/distinctness; council-authorized 0.9 K repair or 0.10 |

None is an excuse to reduce a format/width domain, omit an inverse
obligation, or declare a North Star capability inert. K13 is an example
defect rather than a new machine capability gap. The remaining findings
concern deterministic contract interpretation or delivery. All existing
SQLite, Julia, libc, reversible execution and demo-ladder obligations
remain. Existing beads and cross-repo requests already track the decisions;
no duplicate task or sibling tracker mutation is needed for this pass.

## Publication and combined decisions

[spec/README.md](../../../spec/README.md) requires a full version change
for semantic changes and council review. “0.9 K repair” in these notes
means a proposed use of the existing council repair process, **not
authority to silently amend a published contract**. Without explicit
in-version authorization, the 2qc/sqr semantic additions go to 0.10.
New version prefixes change affected object/interface/closure envelopes,
example identities and consumer pins. A same-version repair with unchanged
source bytes preserves their source identities but still invalidates
incompatible execution/schedule/proof assumptions. `ref@0.9` changes only
if its own catalog bytes/version change; none of these proposals requires
such a rewrite. Assign numeric K entries only at acceptance.

PPC parity and runtime delivery belong to the dialect because they define
LLVM numerical/helper lowering and build inputs. ISA 0.9/0.10 need no
new K for either. Publish the exact proposed dialect text in the next
available maintained patch and mirror it in 0.3; the proposal is not a
private runtime/driver agreement. The new helper protocol is itself
versioned with schema 1, independent of ISA subformat numbers.

For 2qc use the existing concrete invocation-context vocabulary, owner
closure selection and owner-local claim indices. Coordinate with
[pass2 0jq](../2026-11-02-pass2/b-isa-spec-0jq.md); do not create a second
inverse proof owner or replace tuple applicability by root budget equality.
For sqr retain the written source identity and resolve complete wire
operands before K78 primitive matching and scheduling. A parenthesized
indexed list is not currently a legal substitute under §16.2.

## Mixed fat object

Every note cites and applies
[fat-object-scope.md](../2026-11-02-rev2/fat-object-scope.md), binding under
bennett-4ht and advisor 08:45Z (North Star §§2, 3.3, 4.4). A native host
binary remains native; only marked device modules and their required
linked closures become ISA objects. Helper generation, comparison,
certificate inventory and invocation coverage bind those device roots,
with a separate root-relative context [] per root. They do not inventory
native main, host-only argv/envp setup or host-only libc. The shared note
names the exact remaining container/root/certificate association, launch,
budget/handle, tape/run and host-memory mapping changes. This pass neither
implements those mechanisms nor locks them out.

```python
from pathlib import Path
from fractions import Fraction as F
from itertools import product
import hashlib, json, os, re, subprocess, sys

ROOT = Path('/home/tobias/Projects/bennett-workspace/bennett-isa-spec')
WS = ROOT.parent

def pow2(n):
    return F(2**n) if n >= 0 else F(1, 2**(-n))
def decode(bits, fraction_bits, exponent_bits, bias):
    sign = -1 if bits >> (fraction_bits+exponent_bits) else 1
    exp = (bits >> fraction_bits) & ((1 << exponent_bits)-1)
    frac = bits & ((1 << fraction_bits)-1)
    assert exp != (1 << exponent_bits)-1
    sig = frac if exp == 0 else (1 << fraction_bits) | frac
    return sign * sig * pow2((1-bias if exp == 0 else exp-bias)-fraction_bits)
def f64(bits):
    return decode(bits, 52, 11, 1023)
u = pow2(-1074)
h = f64(0x3ff0000000000001)
assert h == 1+pow2(-52)
exact = (h+u)*F(1,2) + f64(0x3fe0000000000001)
assert exact == h+u/2
H = f64(0x0030000000000001)
V = decode(0x3c030000000000001200000000000000, 112, 15, 16383)
assert V == H+u/2 and H > pow2(-1022)
for hi, v in ((h, exact), (H, V)):
    assert (hi/u).denominator == 1
    assert v-hi == hi+u-v == u/2
    assert hi+u-hi == u
# The two high raw significands and the nonzero low significand are odd.
assert (0x3ff0000000000001 & 1) == (0x0030000000000001 & 1) == 1
assert (0 & 1, 1 & 1) == (0, 1)  # proposed common low comparison selects zero
# Both-low-zero subnormal neighbours still use high parity.
assert f64(2)-f64(1) == u and (1 & 1, 2 & 1) == (1, 0)
print('PASS: independent exact PPC FMA/conversion midpoints and parity distinction')

assert block.count('"borrowed":["1"]') == 6
fixed = block.replace('"borrowed":["1"]', '"borrowed":[]')
fixed = fixed.replace('"borrowed":[1]', '"borrowed":[]')
fixed = fixed.replace('1/33554432', '1/16777216')
k13 = json.loads(fixed)
tuples = [k13['row']['before'], k13['row']['after'],

          k13['changed_arg_after'], k13['short_budget_after']]
assert all(t['borrowed'] == [] and t['regions'] == [['2','1']] for t in tuples)
assert k13['widths']['borrowed'] == []
bits = sum(k13['widths']['bundles']) + sum(sum(r) for r in k13['widths']['regions'])
assert bits == 24 and str(F(1, 1 << bits)) == k13['covered_fraction']
assert 2 ^ ((1+2) % 4) == 1 and 2 ^ ((1+1) % 4) == 0
assert (2 ^ 0, 3 ^ 1) == (2, 2)
assert k13['sample_count'] == 4 and k13['distinct_samples'] == 1
print('PASS: all six K13 tuple repairs; 24-bit domain and exact singleton coverage')

# A finite coverage counterexample, not an implementation of the checker.
minimum = (3,0,0,0)
def covers(tk, arg, domain=range(4), token_arg=None):
    return (all(a <= b for a,b in zip(minimum,tk)) and arg in domain
            and (token_arg is None or token_arg == (3,0,0,0)))
assert covers((3,0,0,0),1) and covers((9,0,0,0),1)
assert not covers((2,0,0,0),1)
assert not covers((3,0,0,0),3,range(3))
assert not covers((9,0,0,0),1,token_arg=(4,0,0,0))
contexts = [(3,0,0,0), (2,0,0,0)]
assert [covers(t,1) for t in contexts] == [True,False]
assert {('G','H'), ('H','G')} == {('H','G'), ('G','H')}
print('PASS: qinplace minimum, exact-token, args-domain and two-context counterexamples')

# Proposed wire-request example; arithmetic is independent of any runtime body.

a, b = (1 << 256)+3, 2
assert ((a*b) & mask) == 6
assert a.bit_count() == 3 and (((a*b) & mask).bit_count()) == 2
assert [min(64,257-64*k) for k in range(5)] == [64,64,64,64,1]
entries = [{'op':'mul','width':257}, {'op':'ctpop','width':257}, {'op':'mul','width':257}]
canonical_entries = [{'op':op,'width':w} for op,w in sorted({(e['op'],e['width']) for e in entries})]
assert canonical_entries == [{'op':'ctpop','width':257},{'op':'mul','width':257}]
request = {'schema':'bennett-runtimes-instantiations-1', 'llvm_major':18,
           'target_triple':'x86_64-unknown-linux-musl', 'data_layout':'e-p:64:64',
           'entries':canonical_entries}
canonical = json.dumps(request,sort_keys=True,separators=(',',':')).encode()
print('Proposed request example SHA-256:',hashlib.sha256(canonical).hexdigest())
print('PASS: i257 top-limb arithmetic and canonical request example')

def run(argv, cwd=ROOT, timeout=20, expected=0):
    p = subprocess.run([str(x) for x in argv], cwd=cwd, text=True,
                       capture_output=True, timeout=timeout)
    assert p.returncode == expected, (argv,p.returncode,p.stdout,p.stderr)
    return p

---
name: beads
description: Use when working in a repository that uses bd or Beads for durable project task tracking, issue dependencies, blocker management, multi-session handoff, or shared work memory. Trigger when the user asks to find ready work, claim or close tasks, create follow-up work, inspect blockers, recover project context, or choose between local planning and persistent project tracking.
---

# Beads

Use Beads as the shared project task system. Local plans, scratch files, and personal memories are useful, but they are not the durable source of truth for project work.

## First Step

Run:

```bash
bd prime
```

If that prints nothing, check whether the repository has an active Beads workspace:

```bash
bd where
```

## Preferred Route

Use the `bd` CLI when shell access is available. It is the most compact and direct Beads interface.

## Core CLI Workflow

1. Find work:

```bash
bd ready
bd list --status=open
bd list --status=in_progress
```

2. Inspect before editing:

```bash
bd show <id>
```

3. Claim work atomically:

```bash
bd update <id> --claim
```

4. Create durable follow-up work when implementation reveals new tasks:

```bash
bd create "Short title" --description="Why this exists and what needs to be done" --type=task --priority=2
```

5. Close completed work:

```bash
bd close <id> --reason="Completed"
```

## What Belongs In Beads

Use Beads for:

- shared project tasks
- blockers and dependencies
- discovered follow-up work
- work that must survive thread reset, compaction, or handoff
- status that another person or agent should be able to resume

Use agent-local planning tools only for the current turn's execution checklist. Do not treat them as shared project state.

## Rules

- Do not create markdown TODO files as the source of truth when Beads is available.
- Do not use `bd edit`; it opens an interactive editor. Use `bd update` flags instead.
- Prefer `--json` when parsing `bd` output programmatically.
- If hooks are installed, `bd prime` may already be injected. Run it manually when context is missing.
- Do not auto-close or mutate tasks unless the work is actually complete.

# spec/

`Bennett-ISA-Spec.md` is the normative text: draft 0.8, **accepted by the
three-model council on 2026-10-28 (round 8, all members accept, no blocking
issues, mean 4.17/5)**. Council records and the runner live in the `bennett`
meta repo (`docs/council/isa-2026-10-28/`, `tools/council.py`).

Rules for changing it:

- Every semantic change bumps the full version string (section 1); drafts
  are not envelope-compatible, so the four example hashes in section 16.3
  change with it. Run `python3 reference-interpreter/isa_check.py --repatch`
  then `python3 reference-interpreter/isa_check.py`.
- Surgical edits only; a rewrite has introduced a mechanical error every
  time it was tried.
- Add every new example or stated result to `isa_check.py` before anyone
  grades it.
- Section 17 lists decisions deferred to implementation with their
  defaults; an implementer who must choose something not covered there
  files an issue and the default goes into section 17 first.
- Revisions go back through the council.

1003:| `qinplace` | `qinplace @g @ginv tk x (ins..) (mem..) (args..)`; written `qinplace @g @ginv tk x` when all three lists are empty (and only then) | **Well-formed** iff `@g` and `@ginv` are oracles whose `in` slots are x's width n followed by the widths of `ins` in order, whose `tgt` width is n, whose fault widths are equal (F), whose `mem` slots match the `mem` regions (role `in`, §3.3's matching rule) and whose `args` types are those of `args`; x, the `ins` wires and the effective control context are pairwise disjoint. **Sem**: `t = qalloc any n; f = qalloc any F; oracle_call @g tk (x, ins..) t f (mem..) (args..); qexch x t; oracle_call @ginv tk (x, ins..) t f (mem..) (args..); qfree t; qfree f`, so x ← g(x; ins, mem, args); these are its generated operations, in this order (§11). **Inverse-pair obligation**: under the tuple of `tk`, the call's `args` values and the tuples of the tokens among them, on every basis value of x, of the `ins` wires and of the `mem` cells, both oracles are fault-free and g(·; ins, mem, args) and ginv(·; ins, mem, args) are mutually inverse permutations of the n-bit encoding. It is carried by an `inverse_pairs` entry (§12.3) of the certificate record of the region whose body contains the `qinplace`; an entry `{g: G, ginv: H}` covers both `qinplace @G @H` and `qinplace @H @G` (its claim checks both compositions). An entry **covers** a `qinplace` when its `budget` is componentwise ≤ the tuple of `tk` (success at a tuple implies the same result at every larger one, §5.4), every token-typed argument's tuple equals the entry's `arg_tokens` tuple for that argument (equality: a nested oracle's outcome may change with its budget), and every other `args` value lies in its claim's `args` domain. Its claim is an `exhaustive` claim with `expected: "inverse_pair"`, which the executor verifies as a checker does (once per closure hash, before first use), or a `structural` `inverse_pair_library` claim, which covers when the named claim of the linked library's certificate is such a claim for the same closure hashes of g and ginv (no re-execution; a library claim without an `args` domain covers every value; its proof or evidence label is the library claim's); a `sampled` claim never covers. Before any effect of a CC/R region call, every `qinplace` the callee executes (transitively; its token and `args` are resolved from the call's classical arguments, which reach lifted calls only as classical arguments, §4.2) MUST have a covering entry, else CC trap / R fault `FAULT_UB_TRAP`. The private t and f are not values of the region for §6: the certified pair cleans them, the only cleanup that is not a §6.4 realisation. Under control the allocations are unconditional and every gate of both calls and of the exchange is controlled |

### 12.1 Claims

For `"inverse_pair"` (the claim of an `inverse_pairs` entry, §12.3): for every element (x, ins, mem cells, args) run g and ginv at the entry's tuple, token-typed arguments at their `arg_tokens` tuples, and require both fault-free, ginv(g(x)) = x and g(ginv(x)) = x; the bundles and regions components MUST be the full domain of g's `in` and `mem` slots (the `args` component lists the covered classical values) |

**CLAIM-REPLAY** (K13; b-isa-spec-9uy, from bennett-vm-clw).
Sampled observations remain evidence under the following replay contract.

**Comparison relation.** `exhaustive` and `sampled` claims
MUST name their `subject: { closure_hash, variant, inherited_controls,
schedule_hash? }` and an explicit `expected`; there is no implicit
self-comparison or default expected relation. Without a schedule the
left side is the subject's reference semantics at the bound invocation;
with one it is that invocation's realised operation. `expected: "oracle"`
compares the realised operation to §5.2's reference oracle map and
requires `schedule_hash`. [deps: ISA-5.2, ISA-11, ISA-12.1-P11] `expected: {program: H}` independently runs
the reference semantics of linked closure H at the **same** arguments,
budget and interface tuple; its signature MUST be compatible.
`expected: {table: rows}` compares to the explicit full-tuple rows
below. The checker MUST compare every interface output, preserve inputs
and borrowed systems, and verify clean workspace and exact +1 basis
phase for a permutation assertion (T3); matching a target or fault
projection alone, or a producer's claimed verdict, is insufficient. [deps: ISA-14.3-P3, ISA-5.6]
For a non-permutation realisation, use the existing numerical/channel
claims rather than labeling basis observations a permutation proof. [deps: ISA-12.1-P4, ISA-12.1-P5, ISA-14.2]
K4's `expected: "inverse_pair"` retains its separate two-composition
relation and entry/domain bindings; it is not a sampled proof option.

**Table tuples.** Each table row is `{ before: tuple,
after: tuple }`. A tuple is `{ bundles: ["<unsigned integer>", …],
borrowed: ["<unsigned integer>", …], regions: [["<cell>", …], …] }`.
`bundles` lists inherited control wires first (each width 1), then all
signature bundles in operand order, **including arbitrary initial
oracle target and fault bundles**. `borrowed` lists dirty bundles in
allocation order, with widths fixed by the checked borrow lifetimes;
`regions` lists interface regions in signature order, every cell in
increasing address order, each as an unsigned W-bit integer. All
integers use canonical decimal strings, within the declared widths;
no cells, inputs or unchanged components may be omitted. Workspace is
not an interface component and MUST finish zero. Rows are in domain
enumeration order, one per distinct declared domain element (including
for `sampled`); duplicate/missing/out-of-range rows reject. This favors
an explicit table; `{program: H}` provides the compact relation when a
full table is large. Sampling draws with replacement using the existing
sampler; coverage counts distinct drawn elements, not draws. A mismatch
rejects; success is always `evidence` with that distinct covered fraction
of D, including when the draws happen to cover all of D.

**CLAIM-REPLAY witness**

```json
{
  "invocation":{"args":["2"],"budget":["2","0","0","0"]},
  "row":{
    "before":{"bundles":["1","2","3"],"borrowed":["1"],"regions":[["2","1"]]},
    "after":{"bundles":["1","1","3"],"borrowed":["1"],"regions":[["2","1"]]}
  },
  "wrong_row":{
    "before":{"bundles":["1","2","3"],"borrowed":["1"],"regions":[["2","1"]]},
    "after":{"bundles":["1","2","3"],"borrowed":["1"],"regions":[["2","1"]]}
  },
  "changed_arg_after":{"bundles":["1","0","3"],"borrowed":["1"],"regions":[["2","1"]]},
  "short_budget_after":{"bundles":["1","2","2"],"borrowed":["1"],"regions":[["2","1"]]},
  "sampler":{"algorithm":"xoshiro256**","seed":["1","0","0","0"],"count":4},
  "widths":{"bundles":[2,2,4],"borrowed":[1],"regions":[[8,8]]},
  "sample_count":4,"distinct_samples":1,"covered_fraction":"1/33554432","verdict":"evidence"
}
```

The subject is a two-row oracle body `r = lift.add x a;`
`lift.retval r`, widths x = target = a = 2 and fault = 4, with a preserved
one-bit dirty bundle and an unused two-cell, eight-bit `in` interface
region; no inherited controls. [deps: ISA-5.4, ISA-7.2, ISA-12.1-P8] A legal layout has ptr_width=2, H=2, A_tab=1, S_cells=0,
null_size=1, no globals, the interface mapped at base 1 with extent 2,
heap_base=3 and heap_limit_max=0; the token is (2,0,0,0), and the unused
mapping is retained even though the body has no load/store. [deps: ISA-7.1, ISA-7.2, ISA-5.4]
The **body** does not access memory;
the interface cells remain part of D. [deps: ISA-7.2, ISA-12.2] The reference payload is
(1 + 2) mod 4 = 3: target 2 XOR 3 = 1 and fault 3 XOR 0 = 3.
The singleton declared domain is precisely `row.before`, within the
full 25-bit D (2+2+4+1+16 bits). [deps: ISA-5.4, ISA-7.2, ISA-12.1-P8, ISA-12.2] Four samples of singleton components draw no generator
words and cover one of 33554432 full tuples. [deps: ISA-12.1-P8, ISA-12.1-P13, ISA-12.2] An identity implementation
would agree with itself but MUST fail against `row`; using `wrong_row`
also MUST fail. At argument 1 the target is 2 XOR 2 = 0; at B = 1 the
payload is zero and fault is 3 XOR 1 = 2. A certificate for argument 2,
B = 2 cannot be replayed as either of those invocations. The printed
rows illustrate the relation; bound closure/object hashes still have
to be supplied in an actual certificate, not invented for this witness.

**Invocation contexts.** A subject denotes the set of invocations of
the region `closure_hash`, with the given `variant` and `inherited_
controls`, in the root schedule `schedule_hash`: every invocation of
the region in the root realisation's complete invocation tree —
including invocations that no schedule entry names and defaults alone
cover (§11) — with the classical arguments, budget, `epsilon_share`s and
cleanup realisations resolved for it. An invocation's **context key** is
its concrete invocation chain (§11), outermost first, each element `{
"symbol", "point", "iteration", "instance", "call_index" }` with every
iteration index, fixed-binding key and forward semantic-call occurrence
resolved
and, for an element inside an oracle, `"step"` (1..B) and `"phase"`
(`"fwd"` or `"rev"`) added; contexts are enumerated in increasing
bytewise order of the JCS encodings of their keys (wildcard selectors
play no part). [deps: ISA-3.7-P5, ISA-3.7-P6, ISA-9.2-P1.S3, ISA-11, ISA-11.1-P9]
Every claim bound and every
`error` entry (§12.3) is a **common upper bound over all contexts** of
its key; the multiplicity composition of §12.3 is sound for that reason.

**Diamond-norm SDP** (Watrous, *Theory of Computing* 5 (2009), §4; the
Hermiticity-preserving simplification). Let Φ = Φ_real − Φ_ref be the
difference of the subject's realised channel and the reference channel
(both on the subject's interface, controls included), d_in = d_out = d
the dimension. The **Choi matrix** is J(Φ) = Σ_{i,j} |i⟩⟨j| ⊗ Φ(|i⟩⟨j|)
(input factor first, output factor second, computational basis in
allocation order, **unnormalised**). The witness is a Hermitian Y of
dimension d² and a decimal mu. The checker accepts iff, with every
matrix entry an interval and every test rigorous (an inconclusive test
rejects): Y − J(Φ) ⪰ 0, Y + J(Φ) ⪰ 0, mu·I − Tr_out(Y) ⪰ 0 (partial trace
over the output factor), and mu ≤ `bound`; then ‖Φ‖◇ ≤ mu (the dual of
Watrous's SDP with Y₀ = Y₁ = Y; this is the diamond norm itself, not
half of it). *(non-normative)* With this input-first Choi convention
Tr_out traces the second factor, Tr[(ρ ⊗ I)Y] = Tr[ρ Tr_out(Y)]; a
primal formulation that labels the input factor ρᵀ ranges over the same
set of density matrices, so the bound is unchanged. Encoding: `Y` is a row-major array of entries `[re_lo, re_
hi, im_lo, im_hi]` (decimal strings). **Positivity test.** The checker
proves each of the three matrices **positive definite** by an interval
Cholesky (or Hermitian LDL†) factorisation in which the interval lower
bound of every pivot is strictly positive, and rejects otherwise; no
other test is admitted (nonnegative leading principal minors do not
imply positive semidefiniteness: diag(0, −1)). Producers make witnesses
strictly feasible by replacing Y with Y + δI and mu with mu′ = mu + δ·(d
+ 1) for a small rational δ > 0 (any mu′ > mu + δ·d suffices): then Y ±
J(Φ) ⪰ δI ≻ 0 and, since Tr_out(Y + δI) = Tr_out(Y) + δ·d·I, mu′·I −
Tr_out(Y + δI) ⪰ δI ≻ 0; ‖Φ‖◇ ≤ mu′, which MUST be ≤ `bound`. That
recipe assumes an exactly feasible witness. For a numerically obtained
witness the producer first certifies lower bounds −η₊, −η₋, −η_s on the
smallest eigenvalues of Y − J(Φ), Y + J(Φ) and mu·I − Tr_out(Y), chooses
δ > max(η₊, η₋, η_s) and publishes Y′ = Y + δI and mu′ = mu + (d + 1)·δ:
then Y′ ± J(Φ) ⪰ (δ − η±)I ≻ 0 and mu′·I − Tr_out(Y′) ⪰ (δ − η_s)I ≻ 0.
These margins make the matrices positive definite; they do not make the
interval test conclusive: every pivot enclosure the checker computes at
`precision_bits` MUST still have a strictly positive lower endpoint,
else the checker rejects (no fixed allowance such as 2^−`precision_bits`
bounds the interval error, which depends on d and conditioning). **Interval
arithmetic** at `precision_bits`
means every operation rounds outward to `precision_bits`-bit binary
rationals; transcendental entries (`rx ry rz`) are enclosed by a Taylor
series with the Lagrange remainder bound (§17 item 8 fixes the term
count). A `numerical` or `channel` subject's `schedule_hash` is required:
it names the realisation the claim is about. K13 also requires it for
`expected: "oracle"`; standalone reference relations use the explicit
invocation binding instead.

**Sampling.** For each domain component with range size R = hi − lo + 1,
assemble m = ⌈⌈log₂ R⌉/64⌉ consecutive generator words (least-significant
word first) into an integer, reduce modulo 2^⌈log₂ R⌉, reject and redraw
if ≥ R, add `lo`; components are consumed in enumeration order.

at action A's `entry:0`, the two call edges below are distinct even with
identical arguments, and each prefix is also a round-trippable schedule
path selecting that invocation's descendants. [deps: ISA-4.5-P5, ISA-11-P5, ISA-11.1-P9, ISA-12.1-P11]
For two `lift.call` sites forwarding root token positions 0 and 1 into
lproc f, whose `entry:0` contains one `lift.oracle_call`, the next two
keys distinguish the row instances at outer step 1, even if token values
happen to agree. [deps: ISA-3.7-P5, ISA-3.7-P6, ISA-9.2-P1.S3, ISA-12.1-P11]
The `instance`/`call_index` fields are present in both schedule path
prefixes and context keys; context-only step/phase fields below are
supplied separately as §11 selectors in the schedule form. [deps: ISA-11-P3, ISA-11-P5, ISA-12.1-P11]

### 12.2 Domains

`"domain": { "bundles": [ { "name", "width", "lo", "hi" } ], "borrowed": [ same ], "regions": [ { "name", "cells": "all" | [ { "index", "lo", "hi" } ] } ], "args"?: [ same as bundles ] }`,
bounds inclusive, enumeration order as listed, last component fastest.
`args` (inverse-pair claims only) ranges over classical arguments other
than tokens (whose tuples the entry's `arg_tokens` fixes, §12.3), each
read as an unsigned integer of its width (`ptr`: ptr_width; `f64`: 64,
its bits).
A `proof` label requires the domain to equal D; otherwise the claim is
`evidence` with the covered fraction.
For a borrow-frame obligation, use D_F of §5.6; a region claim over D
does not by itself establish the frame claim. For example, with c
allocated at zero, the single frame `CX(c,b)` over {c=0} × {b=0,1}
is identity on b. The interleaved prefix `CX a c; CX x c; CX a c;
CX x c` clears c only by polynomial algebra; §5.6 instead admits both
c values at entry, so the same single-CX frame fails its obligation.

**Component resolution (K69/K70).** These component-domain rules apply
to table/program/oracle relations; K4 inverse-pair claims retain their
separate two-composition relation and quantified argument bindings.
[deps: ISA-12.1-P7.S8, ISA-12.1-P1.S2, ISA-12.3-P3]
For an oracle the interface bundle groups are `in`, `tgt`, `fault` and its interface regions have role `in`;
for an action the bundle and region roles are `mut`, `in`, with slots
numbered from zero within each declared group. [deps: ISA-5.1-P1, ISA-5.2-P4, ISA-5.3-P1, ISA-9.4-P2]
A `bundles` or `regions` entry's `name` MUST be the subject header's
local name of an interface bundle or region of that category, resolving
to (group, slot); unknown names reject. [deps: ISA-5.1-P1, ISA-9.4-P2, ISA-12.2-P1]
For a subject with c inherited controls, the first c `bundles` entries
instead denote those prepended one-bit controls by position, with
label-only names; all c entries are required, and a singleton range
expresses a fixed control value. [deps: ISA-12.1-P8.S3, ISA-12.1-P7.S1, ISA-12.2-P1]
A region's `cells: "all"` denotes every cell (group, slot, index) in
increasing index order with its full W-bit range, and an explicit cell
list denotes those same cell identities in listed order with the stated
ranges. [deps: ISA-3.3-P19, ISA-12.2-P1.S1]
Entry k of `borrowed` denotes checked borrow lifetime k (zero-based) in
K13's tuple allocation order, independently of any physical storage
reuse; its `name` is only a label, may coincide with any other name and
never denotes an interface component. [deps: ISA-4.3-P2, ISA-5.6-P1, ISA-12.1-P8.S4]
A borrowed position beyond the checked borrow requirement rejects as
ill-formed. [deps: ISA-4.3-P2, ISA-12.1-P8.S4]
A `borrowed` entry MUST NOT discharge an interface bundle or region-cell
coverage obligation, whatever its name. [deps: ISA-4.3-P2, ISA-5.6-P1, ISA-12.1-P8]

**Domain validity and omission (K71/K72).** After resolution, each
component MUST occur at most once, every supplied `width` MUST equal
that component's width, ranges MUST satisfy 0 ≤ lo ≤ hi < 2^width, and
cell indices MUST satisfy 0 ≤ index < 2^A; a violation rejects the
claim as ill-formed. [deps: ISA-3.3-P2.S2, ISA-3.3-P19, ISA-12.2-P1]
Every omitted component is held at zero on every replayed element,
including omitted borrowed positions after the listed entries; workspace
and local regions retain their separate zero/image initialisations.
[deps: ISA-5.2-P4, ISA-5.3-P1, ISA-12.2-P1]
The domain denotes the product of the resolved ranges with those
omitted components fixed at zero, not the full range of an omitted
component. [deps: ISA-5.2-P4, ISA-5.3-P1, ISA-12.2-P1]
For an exhaustive claim its element count n is the product of the range
sizes over individual resolved components, the empty product being 1,
and its covered fraction is exactly n/|D|, with |D| the product of the
full component range sizes (prepended inherited controls included).
[deps: ISA-5.2-P4, ISA-5.3-P1, ISA-12.1-P8, ISA-12.2-P1]

**Coverage and verdicts (K69/K74).** For exhaustive and sampled claims,
`proof` and `evidence` are checker-assigned outcomes recorded in the
checker report against the region closure hash and zero-based `claims`
array index, with the bound invocation or invocation context key where
applicable; they are not producer assertions in the certificate.
[deps: ISA-1.2-P4, ISA-12.1-P7, ISA-12.1-P11, ISA-12.3-P1, ISA-12.3-P3]
An exhaustive table/program/oracle claim receives `proof` iff every
obligation of §12.1 succeeds on every element and its domain equals D
component by
component: every interface bundle, every interface region cell, every
borrowed position and every prepended inherited control occurs exactly
once with its full range. [deps: ISA-12.1-P1, ISA-12.1-P7, ISA-12.1-P8, ISA-5.2-P4, ISA-5.3-P1, ISA-14.3-P3]
This equality is decided over resolved identities and ranges, never
sets of names; an explicit full-range list of every cell covers exactly
the same region domain as `cells: "all"`. [deps: ISA-3.3-P19, ISA-12.2-P1, ISA-12.1-P8]
Successful exhaustive checks on a strict slice receive `evidence` with
n replayed elements and n/|D|; successful sampled checks receive
`evidence` with exactly `count` replayed draws and K13's distinct-draw
fraction, even if it is 1. [deps: ISA-12.1-P1, ISA-12.1-P2, ISA-12.1-P8.S9, ISA-12.1-P8.S10, ISA-12.2-P1]
A supplied `label` of `proof` or `evidence` is only a request: the
checker MUST compute the outcome independently and reject a requested
`proof` when a completed check yields only `evidence`. [deps: ISA-1.2-P4, ISA-12.1-P7.S6, ISA-12.2-P1]
Neither an evidence label nor a requested label discharges an obligation
requiring proof over D. [deps: ISA-5.6-P2, ISA-14.3-P3, ISA-12.3]

**Host limits (K75).** Domain widths, region address width A, cell-list
lengths and sampler `count` have no ISA-imposed upper cap; a checker
MUST NOT treat its host's representability, allocation or replay limits
as domain illegality. [deps: ISA-3.3-P2.S2, ISA-3.3-P19, ISA-12.1-P2, ISA-12.2-P1]
If a checker cannot complete a legal claim on its host, including
materialising an element, enumerating the domain or retaining the
required replay/coverage data, it MUST terminate normally and record
**unchecked (host limit)** for that claim in the same report, citing
§12.2 and the quantity or operation that exceeded the host limit.
[deps: ISA-12.1-P1, ISA-12.1-P2, ISA-12.1-P8, ISA-12.2-P1]
That outcome is distinct from rejection and from either label, supplies
no evidence count or covered fraction for the claim, and discharges no
obligation of §5.6, T3 or §12.3, including when a producer requested a
label. [deps: ISA-1.2-P4, ISA-5.6-P2, ISA-14.3-P3, ISA-12.3]

**COMPONENT-COVERAGE witness**
The following full tuples and resolved identities instantiate the
component rules without a schedule or inherited controls.
[deps: ISA-4.4, ISA-12.1-P8, ISA-12.2-P1]

```json
{
  "mut_names":["t","u"],"widths":[1,1],"borrowed_widths":[1],
  "domain":{"bundles":[{"name":"u","width":1,"lo":0,"hi":1}],"borrowed":[{"name":"t","width":1,"lo":0,"hi":1}],"regions":[]},
  "resolved":[["mut",1],["borrowed",0]],
  "elements":4,"full_elements":8,"covered_fraction":"1/2","verdict":"evidence",
  "renamed_borrowed":"b",
  "counterexample":{
    "before":{"bundles":["1","0"],"borrowed":["0"],"regions":[]},
    "cnot_after":{"bundles":["1","1"],"borrowed":["0"],"regions":[]},
    "identity_after":{"bundles":["1","0"],"borrowed":["0"],"regions":[]}
  },
  "full_bundle_order":["t","u"],"first_mismatch_index":4,
  "region":{"A":1,"W":1,"cells":[{"index":1,"lo":0,"hi":1},{"index":0,"lo":0,"hi":1}],"elements":4,"covers_all":true}
}
```

*(non-normative)* For the one-bit mut t,u action that borrows b,
applies `cx t u` and returns b, compared with identity with the same
borrow requirement, the four elements above agree because omitted t
is zero, while (t,u,b)=(1,0,0) distinguishes the programs; renaming the
borrowed label cannot change the domain or its verdict. [deps: ISA-4.4, ISA-4.3-P2, ISA-12.1-P7, ISA-12.2-P1]
Adding the full t range in the printed full-bundle order exposes the
mismatch at zero-based element 4, rather than yielding proof.
[deps: ISA-4.4, ISA-12.1-P1, ISA-12.2-P1]
The two-cell region's explicit reversed cell list covers all four basis
configurations, while repeating index 1 or using index 2 is ill-formed;
an action region[63,1] with `cells: "all"` and `count: 1` remains legal but has
2^63 bits per element, so an unable host reports unchecked (host limit).
[deps: ISA-3.3-P19, ISA-12.1-P2, ISA-12.2-P1]

### 12.3 Schema and composition

```
  versions; bindings { object_hash, interface_hash, closure_hash (required), reference_program_hash, dialect_mapping_version, target_hash, schedule_hash?, invocation? (K13) }
  producer { name, version, materialisation { llvm_version, pipeline, normaliser_version } }
  regions { <closure_hash>: { kind, signature, bounds, estimate? (K12), frame (required typed-record descriptor, §9.4), resources { name: decimal }, gates_used { gate: { count, scheme?, epsilon? } },
            claims: [ <claim> … ], verification: { oracle: i, borrow_frames: [i], inverse_pairs: [ { g, ginv, claim: i, budget: [B,heap,stack,depth], arg_tokens?: { "<arg>": [B,heap,stack,depth] } } ], unitary: [i], channel: [i],
                                                   fault_rate: { domain, faults_by_code, undefined_excluded, layout_dependent_excluded } },
            cleanup: { "<variant>:<inherited_controls>": [ { path, realisation } ] },
            error:   { "<variant>:<inherited_controls>": { local: { "operator_norm"?: v, "diamond"?: v }, inclusive: { "operator_norm"?: v, "diamond"?: v } } },
            definedness_assumptions: [ids] } }
  composition { multiplicities: { "<hash>:<variant>:<inherited_controls>": n (from the realised schedule) }, epsilon_total: { "diamond": v }, method: "sum_over_multiplicities" }
```

**Invocation binding (K13).** An invocation is
`{ args: [value, …], budget: [B,heap,stack,depth] }`, with every value
resolved before replay and all four budget components explicit even
when zero. `args` is in signature order: `iN` and `ptr` values are
canonical **unsigned decimal strings** of exactly that bit-vector
value (ptr has `ptr_width` bits); `f64` is `0x` followed by exactly 16
lowercase hex digits of its IEEE 754 binary64 bit pattern, most
significant nibble first (so −0 and NaN payloads are not lost); a token
is its four-component tuple of canonical nonnegative decimal strings,
not a process-local token id. Budget components are also canonical
nonnegative decimal strings. Signature types disambiguate encodings;
widths/ranges and every tuple MUST pass §5.4 validation. `params_hash`
is SHA-256 of the UTF-8 JCS **args array**, no trailing LF (§9.2), not
of the whole invocation. Thus equal token tuples have equal parameter
hashes regardless of ids, while +0 and −0 `f64` parameters differ.

Each standalone table/program/structural claim MUST carry its own
`invocation`; it binds
**every** classical argument and budget. Missing values have no implicit
zero/default. Claims with no arguments still bind `args: []` and their
budget. K4 inverse-pair claims instead bind the entry's budget,
`arg_tokens` and quantified `args` domain as already specified; this
rule does not replace that quantification by one chosen argument.
A parameter-uniform structural claim MAY instead bind
`invocation: { args: "all", budget: "all" }`: its checker MUST discharge
the rule's side conditions for every well-typed argument vector and
valid budget, not by replaying a chosen instance. Thus generic library
and adjoint/translation proofs retain their universal scope.
A scheduled certificate MUST carry the root `bindings.invocation`;
its args hash and budget MUST equal the root schedule's bindings, and
all nested arguments and budgets MUST be resolved at each realised
invocation context (§12.1), including default-covered contexts.
Explicit nested bindings, if supplied, MUST equal those resolutions;
a root binding MUST NOT be silently reused for a different callee.
A checker MUST reject absent or conflicting bindings before replay,
and an executor may use a verified result only for the invocation(s)
it actually covers. Reference table/program/structural claims need no
schedule; they prove only their reference relation, not a target
realisation. Scheduled/numerical/channel claims retain the expansion
library gate and all existing context and composition obligations.

**CLAIM-BINDING witness**

```json
{"signature":["i128","f64","token"],"args":["18446744073709551617","0x8000000000000000",["2","0","0","0"]],"params_hash":"9f628a694fa7259bf899bfb677aabce1c084e268a738a40f7f643eb6d8c9c093"}
```

The first argument is > 2^64; the second is binary64 negative zero;
the third is a token tuple, not an id. Replacing negative zero with
`0x0000000000000000` changes `params_hash`; replacing the token by
another valid id of the same tuple does not.

`versions` is the object `{ "isa", "object", "launch", "sched", "cert",
"coverage", "target" }` of the seven header versions. A **variant** is a
nonempty string chosen by the producer (the ISA enumerates none); it is
compared bytewise.

`inclusive(R, key) = local(R, key) + Σ_callees n_c × inclusive(c, key_c)`
**per metric**, keyed like `cleanup` by `<variant>:<inherited_controls>`
(the realised error depends on both); every `local` value is a verified
common upper bound over all invocation contexts of its key in the bound
root schedule (§12.1), so multiplying by n_c is sound; an
`operator_norm` value converts
to `diamond` by ×2 once, at the point it is first combined with a
`diamond` value; `operator_norm` conclusions (T6, controlled unitary
variants) require `operator_norm` bounds throughout. A controlled
unitary variant requires a `numerical` claim; an ε > 0 realisation
compared as a channel requires a `channel` claim. Executable claims
(`numerical`, `channel`, and `exhaustive` or `sampled` with
`expected: "oracle"`, plus table/program comparisons of a realised
operation) bind to `schedule_hash` through their subject. Standalone
reference relations have only the invocation scope defined above (K13).

Checker diagnostic codes other than codes explicitly mandated elsewhere
in this specification are implementation-defined. A checker SHOULD
document its stable code meanings and report the failed normative
obligation and certificate location with a rejection. Such codes are
scoped by checker name and version; their spelling does not alter the
verdict or any executor status/fault code. This convention does not
replace mandated E_SCHEDULE_INFEASIBLE behavior or K55 diagnostics.

## 13. Coverage (coverage 0.9)

`{ "entries": [ { "point": <program-point id>, "source": "<dialect-spec stable id>", "classification": "lowered" | "trapped" | "shimmed", "reason", "fault_code"?, "shim_hash"?, "count" } ] }`.
`fault_code` is present iff `classification` is `trapped`, `shim_hash`
iff it is `shimmed`.

## 14. Formal model

one = const.i64 1
zero = const.i64 0
tk = budget.mint @inc8 three one zero zero
ret
oracle @inc8 (x:wire[8] in) (t:wire[8]) (f:wire[4]) () ()
regs { one:reg[8], r:reg[8] }
^entry():
one = lift.const.i8 1
r = lift.add x one
lift.retval r
```

`@inc8` is example 1's oracle. With b = 1 the token's tuple is (3, 0, 0,
0) and, on basis inputs, t ⊕= (x + 1) mod 256 with f unchanged; with b = 0
it is (2, 0, 0, 0): t unchanged, f ⊕= `FAULT_BUDGET_STEPS` (example 1's
results). Both runs are `COMPLETED` (each under the schedule bound to
its tuple if the object has one, else under the §11 defaults). Reversal
re-applies `@inc8` with the
recorded tuple (an involution), restoring t and f, then revokes the
token. `@overdraw` asks for heap_limit 1 in a memoryless object: the mint
fails `bennett_budget`'s check, the run is `FAILED` with one record
`FAULT_UB_TRAP`, and no token exists.

**Example 7** (`addya`, BLK-3): a parametrised in-place update x ← (x
+ y + a) mod 4 from a declared inverse pair.

```
bennett-object isa=0.9 object=0.9 launch=0.9 sched=0.9 cert=0.9 coverage=0.9 target=0.9 ptr_width=32

section symbols
{"0":{"interface_hash":"0d65d926275688cfebf2831c1de960b03a6b8ea6400866e6d9fef8f393ca2c2e","kind":"oracle","name":"g","signature":{"abi":"0.9","args":["i2"],"fault":4,"in":[2,2],"kind":"oracle","mem":[],"tgt":2,"tier":"q"}},"1":{"interface_hash":"786f1970ca3d78ef3d5de795411ee276286711c89b756872757c24f9bd569cc9","kind":"oracle","name":"ginv","signature":{"abi":"0.9","args":["i2"],"fault":4,"in":[2,2],"kind":"oracle","mem":[],"tgt":2,"tier":"q"}},"2":{"interface_hash":"5999b6d3d2362f8c1971ef6f37b404766aa5c18f6ba1880d767bfde351e47392","kind":"perm_action","name":"addya","signature":{"abi":"0.9","args":["token","i2"],"bounds":[],"in":[2],"kind":"perm_action","mem":[],"mut":[2],"tier":"q"}},"3":{"interface_hash":"562ab7270a0f508d6a2f4a757f3f90964da0b60541bac2c5efa421bad009056a","kind":"cc","name":"run","signature":{"abi":"0.9","kind":"cc","params":["qreg[2]","qreg[2]","token","i2"],"results":[],"tier":"cc"}}}

section functions
oracle @g (x:wire[2] in, y:wire[2] in) (t:wire[2]) (f:wire[4]) () (a:i2)
regs { s:reg[2], r:reg[2] }
^entry():
s = lift.add x y
r = lift.add s a
lift.retval r
oracle @ginv (x:wire[2] in, y:wire[2] in) (t:wire[2]) (f:wire[4]) () (a:i2)
regs { s:reg[2], r:reg[2] }
^entry():
s = lift.sub x y
r = lift.sub s a
lift.retval r
perm_action @addya (x:wire[2]) (y:wire[2] in) () (tk:token, a:i2) ()
^entry():
qinplace @g @ginv tk x (y) () (a)
fn cc @run (x:qreg[2], y:qreg[2], tk:token, a:i2) () () () ()
^entry():
action_call @addya (x) (y) () (tk, a)
ret
```

Each oracle has three rows, so F_B = ((x + y + a) mod 4, 0), resp. ((x − y
− a) mod 4, 0), for B ≥ 3 and (0, `FAULT_BUDGET_STEPS`) for B ≤ 2; for each
(y, a) the two maps are mutually inverse bijections of [0, 4). The object
is completed for execution by a certificate (generated with the 0.9
precheck, which first computes `object_hash`) whose `@addya` region
record carries `verification.inverse_pairs` = `[{"budget":[3,0,0,0],"claim":0,"g":"<closure hash of g>","ginv":"<closure hash of ginv>"}]`
and claim 0 = `{"domain":{"args":[{"hi":3,"lo":0,"name":"a","width":2}],"borrowed":[],"bundles":[{"hi":3,"lo":0,"name":"x","width":2},{"hi":3,"lo":0,"name":"y","width":2}],"regions":[]},"expected":"inverse_pair","kind":"exhaustive"}`
(64 elements). With it and a token whose tuple is ≥ (3, 0, 0, 0), `@run`
maps every basis (x, y) and a ∈ [0, 4) to x′ = (x + y + a) mod 4, y
unchanged, scratch clean: for x = 3, y = 2, a = 1, `@g` gives t = 2, the
exchange gives x = 2, t = 3, and `@ginv`(2; 2, 1) = 3 clears t. The adjoint
maps x to (x − y − a) mod 4; under a control 0 nothing changes. Without the
certificate, or with a token of tuple (2, 0, 0, 0), `@run` traps
`FAULT_UB_TRAP` before any effect.

**Example 8** (`fails`, ISA-7/ISA-8): an R fault after effects, and the
reversal of the failed run.

```

^entry():
